<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that constant-depth quantum circuits with arbitrary one-qubit and unbounded-arity Toffoli gates cannot compute parity with any fixed positive worst-case advantage using polynomially many qubits. Ancillas start in zero, only one output qubit is measured, and all final garbage is unrestricted. This resolves Moore's parity conjecture in the measured-output model.

- **claim-thm-parity** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-parity)): Fix a nonnegative integer $d$, a real number $c\ge1$, and $0<\varepsilon\le1/2$. For all sufficiently large $n$, no circuit of depth at most $d$ on $n$ input qubits and at most $n^c$ total qubits, using arbitrary one-qubit unitaries and unbounded-arity Toffoli gates, computes $\mathop{\mathrm{PARITY}}(x)$ with probability at least $1/2+\varepsilon$ for every $x\in\{0,1\}^n$. Gates in each layer have disjoint supports, ancillas start in $\lvert 0\rangle$, and one output qubit is measured; the other qubits may be discarded.
  - Explanation: Resolves the parity conjecture in the measured-output model by proving that constant-depth quantum circuits with arbitrary one-qubit unitaries and unbounded-arity Toffoli gates cannot compute parity with any fixed positive worst-case advantage using polynomially many total qubits.
- **claim-thm-localization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-localization)): Fix a nonnegative integer $d$ and real numbers $0\le s<t$. For all sufficiently large $N$, every $U\in\mathcal U_d(N)$ and every pair of counts $M,D$ on $\mathcal H_N$ satisfy $$\label{eq:localization} \left\lVert [M\ge N^t]\,U[D=0]U^\dagger[M=0]\right\rVert \le \exp(-N^s).$$ The sufficient lower bound on $N$ depends only on $d,s,t$, not on the circuit, the counted subsets, or their reference vectors.
  - Explanation: Establishes uniform operator-norm localization: conjugating any product projection by a constant-depth circuit in the reflection normal form produces exponentially small transition norm between the zero-mismatch subspace and high-mismatch subspaces, uniformly over circuits, counted subsets, and reference vectors.
- **claim-lem-polynomial** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-polynomial)): Fix real numbers $0<a<b$ and $u>(a+b)/2$. For every sufficiently large integer $N$, there is a real polynomial $p$ such that $$\label{eq:polynomial-bounds} \deg p\le N^u, \qquad W_N(p)\le \exp(N^u), \qquad p(0)=1,$$ and $$\label{eq:polynomial-decay} \|p(x)\|\le \exp(-N^a) \qquad\text{for every integer }1\le x\le \lceil N^b\rceil.$$ The threshold for $N$ depends only on $a,b,u$. No restriction $b\le1$ is imposed.
  - Explanation: Constructs a low-degree real polynomial combining exact roots at the first integer points with a rescaled Chebyshev polynomial to approximate the zero spectral projection of a count operator while bounding the weighted coefficient norm.
- **claim-lem-base** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-base)): For every fixed $0\le s<t$, the statement $\mathcal B_0(s,t)$ holds.
  - Explanation: Establishes the depth-zero base case of operator localization by bounding the product-projection overlap and applying Markov inequality to independent Bernoulli mismatch variables.
- **claim-lem-propagation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-propagation)): Fix $d\ge1$ and assume $\mathcal B_{d-1}(s,t)$ for every fixed pair $0\le s<t$. For fixed $\alpha,\sigma\ge0$ and $\beta>\max\{\alpha,\sigma\}$, all sufficiently large $N$ satisfy $$\label{eq:low-count-propagation} \left\lVert [M\ge N^\beta]B[M<N^\alpha]\right\rVert \le \exp(-N^\sigma)$$ uniformly for every count $M$ and every $B=VP_*V^\dagger$, where $V\in\mathcal U_{d-1}(N)$ and $P_*$ is a product projection.
  - Explanation: Extends the preceding-depth localization hypothesis from a single zero-mismatch projection to the entire subspace of low mismatch counts by summing over all mismatch subsets below the threshold.
- **claim-lem-small-support** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-small-support)): Fix $d\ge1$ and assume $\mathcal B_{d-1}(s,t)$ for every fixed pair $0\le s<t$. For fixed $u,w\ge0$ and $t>\max\{u,w\}$, all sufficiently large $N$ satisfy $$\label{eq:small-support} \left\lVert [M\ge N^t]UAU^\dagger[M=0]\right\rVert \le\exp(-N^w)$$ uniformly for every $U\in\mathcal U_d(N)$, every count $M$, and every product projection $A$ supported on at most $N^u$ qubits.
  - Explanation: Proves localization at depth d for product projections supported on at most N^u qubits by expanding the meeting reflection gates in the first layer into sums of three depth-(d-1) conjugated projections separated by intermediate mismatch cutoffs.
- **claim-lem-backward** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-backward)): Fix $d\ge1$, and assume $\mathcal B_{d-1}(s,t)$ for every fixed $0\le s<t$. Let $s,t,b$ be fixed real numbers with $$0\le s<t<b<2t-s.$$ If $\mathcal B_d(t,b)$ holds, then $\mathcal B_d(s,t)$ holds.
  - Explanation: Executes one backward scale-induction step at depth d: assuming depth-(d-1) localization and depth-d localization at a higher mismatch exponent pair (t,b), it establishes depth-d localization at (s,t) via polynomial approximation and unitary inversion.
- **claim-cor-state-preparation** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-state-preparation)): Fix a nonnegative integer $d$, $c\ge1$, $a>0$, and $0<\delta<1$. For all sufficiently large $n$, every $n$-qubit reduced state $\rho$ prepared from zero inputs by an allowed depth-at-most-$d$ circuit on at most $n^c$ total qubits satisfies $$\mathcal F_n(\rho)<n^{-a}.$$ Moreover, for every integer $k$ with $n^\delta\le k\le n/2$, $$\operatorname{TD}(\rho,\lvert D_k^n\rangle\!\langle D_k^n\rvert)>\frac1{80k}.$$ The remaining qubits may be discarded in arbitrary final states.
  - Explanation: Establishes state-preparation obstructions for constant-depth, polynomial-qubit circuits starting from zero inputs: the felinity of any reduced n-qubit output state decays faster than any inverse power of n, and the trace distance to any Dicke state of intermediate Hamming weight k is bounded below by 1/(80k).
- **claim-cor-quantum-lmn** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-quantum-lmn)): For every fixed nonnegative integer $d$, there is a positive function $\gamma_d$ on the positive integers, depending only on $d$, such that $\gamma_d(k)/k^A\to\infty$ for every fixed $A>0$, and every allowed depth-at-most-$d$ circuit $C$ on $n$ inputs and $N$ total qubits, every designated output $t$, and every integer $1\le k\le n$ satisfy $$W_{\ge k}[f_C]\le\frac{N}{\gamma_d(k)}.$$ In particular, fix $c\ge1$, $0<\delta\le1$, and $B>0$. For all sufficiently large $n$, uniformly over these circuits with $N\le n^c$, $$W_{\ge\lceil n^\delta\rceil}[f_C]\le n^{-B}.$$ The sufficient threshold depends only on $d,c,\delta,B$.
  - Explanation: Proves qualitative scalar-output Fourier concentration for constant-depth quantum circuits: the total squared Fourier weight above degree k is bounded by N divided by a superpolynomially growing function of k, yielding superpolynomial decay above polynomial degree cutoffs when N is polynomial in n.
- **claim-cor-symmetric-functions** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-symmetric-functions)): Let $(f_n)$ be a symmetric Boolean family satisfying either $\rho(f_n)\ge n^\delta$ for some fixed $\delta>0$, or $\widetilde{\deg}_{1/3}(f_n)=\Omega(n^{1/2+\eta})$ for some fixed $\eta>0$, for all sufficiently large $n$. For every fixed $\gamma>0$, no nonuniform family in the circuit model of [thm:parity](#thm:parity), with fixed depth and polynomially many total qubits, computes $f_n$ with probability at least $1/2+1/(\log n)^\gamma$ on every input for all sufficiently large $n$. In particular, this excludes strict majority $\operatorname{MAJ}_n(x)=\mathbf 1\{\|x\|>n/2\}$, whose transition radius is $\lceil n/2\rceil$.
  - Explanation: Proves that symmetric Boolean function families with polynomial transition radius or polynomial approximate degree above square-root n (including strict majority) cannot be computed with inverse-polylogarithmic worst-case advantage by constant-depth, polynomial-qubit circuits.
- **claim-cor-majority-average** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-majority-average)): For every fixed $\delta>0$, no nonuniform family $(C_n)$ in the circuit model of [thm:parity](#thm:parity), with fixed depth and polynomially many total qubits, satisfies $$2^{-n}\sum_{x\in\{0,1\}^n} f_{C_n}(x)(-1)^{\operatorname{MAJ}_n(x)}\ge1-n^{-\delta}$$ for all sufficiently large $n$. Here $f_{C_n}(x)\in[-1,1]$ is the expected measured sign: the probability of output zero minus the probability of output one. Equivalently, no such family computes strict majority with average success at least $1-\tfrac12 n^{-\delta}$, where the average is over uniform inputs and the output measurement.
  - Explanation: Rules out high average-case accuracy for strict majority: no constant-depth, polynomial-qubit circuit family can achieve expected sign correlation at least 1 - n^{-delta} (or average success probability at least 1 - (1/2)n^{-delta}) on uniformly random inputs.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Boolean parity function PARITY(x)** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a computational-basis string $x=(x_1,\ldots,x_n)\in\{0,1\}^n$, its value is $\mathop{\mathrm{PARITY}}(x)=x_1\oplus\cdots\oplus x_n$.
- **Worst-case advantage epsilon for parity** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For $0<\varepsilon\le1/2$, computing parity with worst-case advantage $\varepsilon$ means giving the correct answer with probability at least $1/2+\varepsilon$ for every $x$.
- **Quantum circuit class QAC^0** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The class $\mathrm{QAC}^0$ uses constant depth and polynomially many total qubits.
- **Mismatch count operator M** ([source](#sec:localization)): A *count* is an operator $$\label{eq:count} M=\sum_{i\in S}\bigl(I-\lvert v_i\rangle\!\langle v_i\rvert\bigr)_i, \qquad S\subseteq\{1,\ldots,N\},\quad \left\lVert v_i\right\rVert=1.$$
- **Product projection** ([source](#sec:localization)): A *product projection* is a tensor product of rank-one one-qubit projections on a subset, with identity on the complement.
- **Reflection normal-form circuit class U_d(N)** ([source](#eq:normal-form)): Let $\mathcal U_d(N)$ be the set of unitaries $$\label{eq:normal-form} U=(L_dR_d)\cdots(L_1R_1)L_0,$$ where each $L_j$ is a product of one-qubit unitaries and each $R_j$ is a layer of gates $I-2A$ with disjoint supports, with $A$ a product projection on the gate’s qubits.
- **Weighted coefficient norm W_N(f)** ([source](#sec:polynomial)): For a polynomial $f(x)=\sum_k c_kx^k$, define its weighted coefficient norm by $$W_N(f)=\sum_k\|c_k\|N^k.$$
- **Felinity F_n(rho)** ([source](#sec:state-preparation)): For an $n$-qubit density operator $\rho$, write $$p_y=\langle y\rvert\rho\lvert y\rangle,\qquad \mathcal F_n(\rho)=2\sum_{y\in\{0,1\}^n}p_y p_{\bar y},$$ where $\bar y$ is the bitwise complement of $y$. This is the *felinity* of $\rho$ [[GGJ2026](#ref-GGJ2026), Definition 2.1].
- **Dicke state D_k^n and trace distance TD** ([source](#sec:state-preparation)): Also write $$\lvert D_k^n\rangle=\binom{n}{k}^{-1/2}\sum_{\|y\|=k}\lvert y\rangle, \qquad \operatorname{TD}(\rho,\sigma)=\tfrac12\left\lVert \rho-\sigma\right\rVert_1$$ for the weight-$k$ Dicke state and trace distance, respectively.
- **Squared Fourier tail weight W_{>= k}[f_C]** ([source](#pm-h-14)): Write $W_{\ge k}[f_C]=\sum_{\|S\|\ge k}\|\widehat f_C(S)\|^2$.
- **Transition radius rho(f_n) of a symmetric Boolean function** ([source](#pm-h-15)): For a symmetric $f_n:\{0,1\}^n\to\{0,1\}$, let $f_{n,i}$ be its value on inputs of Hamming weight $i$, and define its transition radius by $$\rho(f_n)=\max_{\substack{0\le i<n\\f_{n,i}\ne f_{n,i+1}}} \min\{i+1,n-i\},$$ with $\rho(f_n)=0$ for a constant function.
- **Approximate degree deg_tilde_{1/3}(f_n)** ([source](#pm-h-15)): Write $\widetilde{\deg}_{1/3}(f_n)$ for the least total degree of a real polynomial approximating $f_n$ pointwise within $1/3$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| qty-parity-success-threshold | worst-case parity success probability threshold | ≥ | $1/2+\varepsilon$ | Fixed depth d >= 0, polynomial degree c >= 1, worst-case advantage 0 < epsilon <= 1/2, sufficiently large input length n. |
| qty-total-qubit-bound | total qubits in polynomial QAC^0 circuit | ≤ | $n^c$ | Fixed real exponent c >= 1, input size n. |
| qty-localization-operator-norm | operator norm of conjugated product projection transition from zero mismatches to at least N^t mismatches | ≤ | $\exp(-N^s)$ | Fixed depth d >= 0, fixed real exponents 0 <= s < t, sufficiently large total qubit count N depending only on d, s, t. |
| qty-polynomial-degree | degree of approximating polynomial p | ≤ | $N^u$ | Fixed real exponents 0 < a < b and u > (a+b)/2, sufficiently large integer N. |
| qty-polynomial-weighted-norm | weighted coefficient norm W_N(p) of approximating polynomial | ≤ | $\exp(N^u)$ | Fixed real exponents 0 < a < b and u > (a+b)/2, sufficiently large integer N. |
| qty-polynomial-integer-decay | magnitude \|p(x)\| of approximating polynomial on positive integers up to ceil(N^b) | ≤ | $\exp(-N^a)$ | Integers 1 <= x <= ceil(N^b), fixed 0 < a < b, sufficiently large N. |
| qty-low-count-propagation | low-count propagation operator norm \|\|[M >= N^beta] B [M < N^alpha]\|\| | ≤ | $\exp(-N^\sigma)$ | Fixed depth d >= 1, fixed exponents alpha, sigma >= 0 and beta > max{alpha, sigma}, sufficiently large N. |
| qty-small-support-localization | small-support conjugated projection transition norm \|\|[M >= N^t] U A U^dagger [M=0]\|\| | ≤ | $\exp(-N^w)$ | Fixed depth d >= 1, fixed exponents u, w >= 0 and t > max{u, w}, support size k <= N^u, sufficiently large N. |
| qty-reflection-expansion-coeff-sum | sum of absolute scalar coefficients in two-sided reflection expansion over k gates | ≤ | $9^k$ | Support size 0 <= k <= N^u. |
| qty-felinity-bound | felinity F_n(rho) of n-qubit reduced state prepared by shallow circuit | < | $n^{-a}$ | Fixed depth d >= 0, total qubit exponent c >= 1, decay exponent a > 0, sufficiently large n. |
| qty-dicke-trace-distance | trace distance TD(rho, \|D_k^n><D_k^n\|) to weight-k Dicke state | > | $\frac1{80k}$ | Fixed d >= 0, c >= 1, 0 < delta < 1, Hamming weight n^delta <= k <= n/2, sufficiently large n. |
| qty-fourier-tail-general | squared Fourier tail weight W_{>= k}[f_C] of expected circuit output sign | ≤ | $\frac{N}{\gamma_d(k)}$ | Fixed depth d >= 0, degree cutoff 1 <= k <= n, total qubits N >= n. |
| qty-fourier-tail-polynomial-cutoff | squared Fourier tail weight W_{>= ceil(n^delta)}[f_C] at polynomial cutoff | ≤ | $n^{-B}$ | Fixed d >= 0, c >= 1, 0 < delta <= 1, B > 0, sufficiently large n. |
| qty-symmetric-worst-case-threshold | excluded worst-case success probability for symmetric Boolean functions with polynomial transition radius | ≥ | $1/2+1/(\log n)^\gamma$ | Fixed depth d >= 0, polynomial total qubits, fixed delta > 0 or eta > 0, fixed gamma > 0, sufficiently large n. |
| qty-majority-average-success | excluded average-case success probability for strict majority over uniform inputs | ≥ | $1-\tfrac12 n^{-\delta}$ | Fixed depth d >= 0, polynomial total qubits, fixed delta > 0, sufficiently large n. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-1** [claim](#stmt-thm-localization) / [proof](#sec:localization): Embed the physical circuit into the reflection normal form class U_d(N) where each multi-qubit Toffoli gate is expressed as I - 2A for a product projection A, and establish closure under Hermitian conjugation at the same depth. -> Unitary representation U in U_d(N) with U^dagger in U_d(N)
- **step-2** [claim](#stmt-lem-polynomial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-polynomial): Construct an interpolating-Chebyshev polynomial p(x) by multiplying an exact integer root product F(x) on {1, ..., r} with an affine-rescaled Chebyshev polynomial T_ell, bounding both integer-point values on [1, ceil(N^b)] and the weighted coefficient norm W_N(p). -> Real polynomial p with deg p <= N^u, W_N(p) <= exp(N^u), p(0)=1, and \|p(x)\| <= exp(-N^a) on {1, ..., ceil(N^b)}
- **step-3** [claim](#stmt-lem-base) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-base): Prove the depth-zero base case B_0(s,t) by computing the unnormalized product-state overlap and applying Markov inequality to 2^K for the sum K of independent Bernoulli mismatch indicators. -> Depth-zero localization bound \|\|[M >= N^t] U[D=0]U^dagger [M=0]\|\| <= exp(-N^s)
- **step-4** [claim](#stmt-lem-propagation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-propagation): Decompose the low-mismatch spectral projection [M < N^alpha] into a sum of exact mismatch pattern projections [M^F = 0] over subsets \|F\| < N^alpha and apply the depth-(d-1) localization hypothesis with the triangle inequality. -> Low-count propagation bound \|\|[M >= N^beta] B [M < N^alpha]\|\| <= exp(-N^sigma)
- **step-5** [claim](#stmt-lem-small-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-small-support): Peel off the first reflection layer R_1 for a projection A supported on k <= N^u qubits by expanding the at most k intersecting reflections on both sides of A into at most 9^k products of three depth-(d-1) conjugated projections, bounding transitions via two intermediate mismatch cutoffs. -> Small-support localization bound \|\|[M >= N^t] U A U^dagger [M=0]\|\| <= exp(-N^w)
- **step-6** [claim](#stmt-lem-backward) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-backward): Square the transition operator norm \|\|HQP\|\|^2 = \|\|HQPQH\|\|, replace the left Q = U[D=0]U^dagger by p(UDU^dagger) to bound Hp(C)P via small-support localization, and split the residual (Q - p(C))PQ at spectral threshold N^b where the high-C tail is bounded by applying B_d(t,b) to U^dagger. -> Backward step implication B_d(t,b) => B_d(s,t) for 0 <= s < t < b < 2t - s
- **step-7** [claim](#stmt-thm-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-backward-2): Define the finite scale chain s_{i+1} = t_i, t_{i+1} = 2t_i - s_i - 3*eta with eta = (t-s)^2 / 100, reaching t_J > 1 in J <= ceil(2/(t-s)) steps where [M >= N^{t_J}] = 0, and induct backward from J to 0 using step-6. -> Universal operator-norm localization B_d(s,t) for every fixed d >= 0 and 0 <= s < t
- **step-8** [claim](#stmt-thm-parity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-backward-3): Express the parity Fourier coefficient of the output-zero acceptance probability p_0(x) by character orthogonality as the average over h in {0,1}^n of matrix entries <xi_{bar h}, 0 \| W^dagger O W \| xi_h, 0> between complementary Hadamard product states at mismatch distance n, and bound each entry by exp(-N^s) using localization with 0 < s < t < 1/c. -> Contradiction between worst-case parity advantage >= epsilon and upper bound exp(-N^s) -> 0
- **step-9** [claim](#stmt-cor-state-preparation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-state-preparation): Bound complementary basis string probabilities sqrt(p_y p_{bar y}) <= exp(-N^s) by localization to prove F_n(rho) < n^{-a}, and apply a single-layer block product-reflection circuit to convert a Dicke state into a high-felinity state on ell = floor(k / log k) qubits, yielding the trace distance lower bound 1/(80k). -> Felinity bound F_n(rho) < n^{-a} and Dicke state trace distance lower bound TD > 1/(80k)
- **step-10** [claim](#stmt-cor-quantum-lmn) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-quantum-lmn): Represent Fourier coefficients hat{f}_C(S) as averages of transitions between orthonormal shifted Hadamard states \|xi_{h oplus 1_S}, 0> at mismatch count \|S\|, apply Bessel inequality and localization to bound W_{>= k}[f_C], and take a supremum over circuit sizes to define the superpolynomial growth function gamma_d(k). -> Scalar-output Fourier tail concentration bounds W_{>= k}[f_C] <= N / gamma_d(k) and W_{>= ceil(n^delta)}[f_C] <= n^{-B}
- **step-11** [claim](#stmt-cor-symmetric-functions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-symmetric-functions): Reduce worst-case computation of symmetric Boolean functions with polynomial transition radius or approximate degree >= Omega(n^{1/2+eta}) to exact constant-depth quantum fanout, and conjugate by Hadamard gates to contradict the parity lower bound. -> Impossibility of worst-case advantage 1/(log n)^gamma for symmetric functions with polynomial transition radius (including strict majority)
- **step-12** [claim](#stmt-cor-majority-average) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-majority-average): Combine the L2 distance bound \|\|f_{C_n} - g_n\|\|_2^2 <= 2n^{-delta_0} from high average correlation with the polynomial Fourier tail lower bound of strict majority at odd cutoff k_n, contradicting the superpolynomial Fourier tail upper bound of step-10. -> Impossibility of average-case sign correlation >= 1 - n^{-delta} with strict majority

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **P3-I1**: Constant-depth quantum circuits with arbitrary one-qubit unitaries and unbounded-arity Toffoli gates on polynomially many total qubits cannot compute parity with any fixed positive worst-case advantage in the measured-output model.
- **P3-I2**: Conjugating any product projection by a constant-depth circuit in the product-state reflection model yields superpolynomially small operator-norm transitions from zero mismatches to large mismatch counts uniformly over circuits and counts.
- **P3-I3**: The Chebyshev-interpolating polynomial with exact integer roots satisfies unit normalization at zero, vanishes on the first r positive integers, decays exponentially on integer points up to the upper cutoff, and obeys the weighted coefficient norm bound.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **P3-A1**: Apply the constant-depth parity and symmetric-function lower bounds to quantum circuits equipped with primitive unbounded-fanout gates, intermediate measurements, or postselection. -> Unbounded fanout is equivalent to coherent parity under Hadamard conjugation and enables constant-depth computation of parity and majority, invalidating the lower bound.
- **P3-A2**: Extrapolate the parity impossibility theorem to constant-depth quantum circuits with superpolynomially many ancillary qubits. -> Choosing exponents 0 < s < t < 1/c requires a fixed polynomial exponent c with N <= n^c; superpolynomial ancilla counts break the transition from operator localization to parity decay.
- **P3-A3**: Invoke the high-average-accuracy majority obstruction to rule out constant-depth circuits achieving 1 - 1/polylog(n) average correlation with strict majority. -> The Fourier concentration contradiction at polynomial degree cutoffs only excludes correlation at least 1 - n^{-\delta} for fixed \delta > 0.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Lemma 1 provides an explicit finite polynomial construction p(x) combining an integer-root product F(x) with a normalized Chebyshev factor T_ell((m+r-2x)/(m-r)) / T_ell((m+r)/(m-r)) and explicit finite algebraic bounds on its integer evaluations and weighted coefficient norm W_N(p). This harness certifies those finite algebraic polynomial identities and bounds on small integer parameters; it does not certify the asymptotic quantum circuit lower bounds.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with integer parameters r, m, N.
# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= r <= 3, 2 * r <= m <= 8, 1 <= N <= 8.
# OUTPUTS: JSON object with exact degree, ell, p0, roots_zero, denom_ok, tail_ok, wn_ok, wn, max_tail, or error on invalid input.
# TIME: O((r + ell)^2) exact rational polynomial operations where ell = ceil(2 * sqrt(m * r) * log(m)) <= 20.
# SPACE: O(r + ell) exact rational coefficients.
# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpq_poly and fmpq exact rationals; arb ball arithmetic with unique_fmpz certification for ell.
# FAILURE: inadmissible or malformed input returns {"error": "preconditions"}.
import json, sys
from flint import arb, fmpq, fmpq_poly

def admitted(x):
    if not isinstance(x, dict) or set(x) != {'r', 'm', 'N'}:
        return False
    if any(type(v) is not int for v in x.values()):
        return False
    r, m, N = x['r'], x['m'], x['N']
    return 1 <= r <= 3 and 2 * r <= m <= 8 and 1 <= N <= 8

try:
    req = json.load(sys.stdin)
    if not admitted(req):
        print(json.dumps({'error': 'preconditions'}, sort_keys=True))
    else:
        r, m, N = req['r'], req['m'], req['N']
        ell_ball = (arb(2) * (arb(m * r).sqrt()) * (arb(m).log())).ceil()
        ell = int(ell_ball.unique_fmpz())
        x_poly = fmpq_poly([0, 1])
        F_poly = fmpq_poly([1])
        for j in range(1, r + 1):
            F_poly = F_poly * (fmpq_poly([1]) - x_poly / fmpq(j))
        z_poly = fmpq_poly([fmpq(m + r, m - r), fmpq(-2, m - r)])
        T_prev, T_curr = fmpq_poly([1]), z_poly
        for _ in range(2, ell + 1):
            T_prev, T_curr = T_curr, fmpq(2) * z_poly * T_curr - T_prev
        T_poly = fmpq_poly([1]) if ell == 0 else (z_poly if ell == 1 else T_curr)
        denom = T_poly(fmpq(0))
        p_poly = (F_poly * T_poly) / denom
        deg = p_poly.degree()
        coeffs = [p_poly[k] for k in range(deg + 1)]
        wn = sum(abs(c) * (fmpq(N) ** k) for k, c in enumerate(coeffs))
        vals = [p_poly(fmpq(k)) for k in range(m + 1)]
        roots_zero = all(vals[k] == 0 for k in range(1, r + 1))
        max_tail = max(abs(vals[k]) for k in range(r, m + 1))
        denom_ok = bool(denom >= fmpq(m ** (4 * r), 2))
        tail_ok = bool(max_tail <= fmpq(2) / (fmpq(m) ** (3 * r)))
        wn_ok = bool(wn <= ((1 + fmpq(N)) ** r) * ((11 * (1 + fmpq(N))) ** ell))
        out = {
            'degree': deg,
            'denom_ok': denom_ok,
            'ell': ell,
            'max_tail': str(max_tail),
            'p0': str(vals[0]),
            'roots_zero': roots_zero,
            'tail_ok': tail_ok,
            'wn': str(wn),
            'wn_ok': wn_ok
        }
        print(json.dumps(out, sort_keys=True))
except Exception:
    print(json.dumps({'error': 'preconditions'}, sort_keys=True))
```

    "oracle_code": "# Independent idiom: SymPy symbolic Chebyshev polynomial expansion and exact Rational evaluation.\nimport json as codec\nimport sys as system\nimport sympy as sp\n\ndef valid_payload(data):\n    if not isinstance(data, dict) or sorted(data) != ['N', 'm', 'r']:\n        return False\n    if any(type(data[k]) is not int for k in data):\n        return False\n    rad, top, size = data['r'], data['m'], data['N']\n    return 1 <= rad <= 3 and 2 * rad <= top <= 8 and 1 <= size <= 8\n\npayload = codec.loads(open(system.argv[1]).read())\nraw_out = open(system.argv[2]).read().strip()\nif not raw_out:\n    raise SystemExit(2)\nobserved = codec.loads(raw_out)\nexpected_tag = codec.loads(system.argv[3])\nis_ok = valid_payload(payload)\nif not is_ok:\n    if expected_tag != {'error': 'preconditions'} or observed != {'error': 'preconditions'}:\n        raise SystemExit(3)\n    raise SystemExit(0)\n\nrad, top, size = payload['r'], payload['m'], payload['N']\ndeg_cheb = int(sp.ceiling(2 * sp.sqrt(top * rad) * sp.log(top)))\nu = sp.Symbol('u')\nroot_part = sp.Integer(1)\nfor idx in range(1, rad + 1):\n    root_part = root_part * (1 - sp.Rational(1, idx) * u)\naffine_arg = sp.Rational(top + rad, top - rad) - sp.Rational(2, top - rad) * u\ncheb_num = sp.chebyshevt(deg_cheb, affine_arg)\ncheb_den = sp.chebyshevt(deg_cheb, sp.Rational(top + rad, top - rad))\nfull_poly = sp.Poly(sp.expand(root_part * cheb_num / cheb_den), u, domain='QQ')\nall_coeffs = full_poly.all_coeffs()[::-1]\nweighted_sum = sum(abs(c) * (sp.Integer(size) ** pwr) for pwr, c in enumerate(all_coeffs))\ngrid_vals = [full_poly.eval(sp.Integer(pt)) for pt in range(top + 1)]\ntail_peak = max(abs(grid_vals[pt]) for pt in range(rad, top + 1))\nden_check = bool(cheb_den >= sp.Rational(top ** (4 * rad), 2))\ntail_check = bool(tail_peak <= sp.Rational(2, top ** (3 * rad)))\nwn_check = bool(weighted_sum <= ((1 + sp.Integer(size)) ** rad) * ((11 * (1 + sp.Integer(size))) ** deg_cheb))\nif not (grid_vals[0] == 1 and all(grid_vals[pt] == 0 for pt in range(1, rad + 1)) and den_check and tail_check and wn_check):\n    raise SystemExit(4)\ntruth = {\n    'degree': rad + deg_cheb,\n    'denom_ok': den_check,\n    'ell': deg_cheb,\n    'max_tail': str(tail_peak),\n    'p0': str(grid_vals[0]),\n    'roots_zero': True,\n    'tail_ok': tail_check,\n    'wn': str(weighted_sum),\n    'wn_ok': wn_check\n}\nif observed != truth or expected_tag != truth:\n    raise SystemExit(5)\nraise SystemExit(0)\n",
    "output_contract": "JSON object reporting exact degree r + ell, certified Chebyshev degree ell, p(0) = 1, exact vanishing on integers 1..r, denominator bound >= m^(4r)/2, tail decay <= 2*m^(-3r) on integers r..m, and weighted norm W_N(p) <= (1+N)^r (11(1+N))^ell.",
    "reason": "Lemma 1 provides an explicit finite polynomial construction p(x) combining an integer-root product F(x) with a normalized Chebyshev factor T_ell((m+r-2x)/(m-r)) / T_ell((m+r)/(m-r)) and explicit finite algebraic bounds on its integer evaluations and weighted coefficient norm W_N(p). This harness certifies those finite algebraic polynomial identities and bounds on small integer parameters; it does not certify the asymptotic quantum circuit lower bounds.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON object with integer parameters r, m, N.\n# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= r <= 3, 2 * r <= m <= 8, 1 <= N <= 8.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON object with exact degree, ell, p0, roots_zero, denom_ok, tail_ok, wn_ok, wn, max_tail, or error on invalid input.\n# TIME: O((r + ell)^2) exact rational polynomial operations where ell = ceil(2 * sqrt(m * r) * log(m)) <= 20.\n# SPACE: O(r + ell) exact rational coefficients.\n# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpq_poly and fmpq exact rationals; arb ball arithmetic with unique_fmpz certification for ell.\n# FAILURE: inadmissible or malformed input returns {\"error\": \"preconditions\"}.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import arb, fmpq, fmpq_poly\n\ndef admitted(x):\n    if not isinstance(x, dict) or set(x) != {'r', 'm', 'N'}:\n        return False\n    if any(type(v) is not int for v in x.values()):\n        return False\n    r, m, N = x['r'], x['m'], x['N']\n    return 1 <= r <= 3 and 2 * r <= m <= 8 and 1 <= N <= 8\n\ntry:\n    req = json.load(sys.stdin)\n    if not admitted(req):\n        print(json.dumps({'error': 'preconditions'}, sort_keys=True))\n    else:\n        r, m, N = req['r'], req['m'], req['N']\n        ell_ball = (arb(2) * (arb(m * r).sqrt()) * (arb(m).log())).ceil()\n        ell = int(ell_ball.unique_fmpz())\n        x_poly = fmpq_poly([0, 1])\n        F_poly = fmpq_poly([1])\n        for j in range(1, r + 1):\n            F_poly = F_poly * (fmpq_poly([1]) - x_poly / fmpq(j))\n        z_poly = fmpq_poly([fmpq(m + r, m - r), fmpq(-2, m - r)])\n        T_prev, T_curr = fmpq_poly([1]), z_poly\n        for _ in range(2, ell + 1):\n            T_prev, T_curr = T_curr, fmpq(2) * z_poly * T_curr - T_prev\n        T_poly = fmpq_poly([1]) if ell == 0 else (z_poly if ell == 1 else T_curr)\n        denom = T_poly(fmpq(0))\n        p_poly = (F_poly * T_poly) / denom\n        deg = p_poly.degree()\n        coeffs = [p_poly[k] for k in range(deg + 1)]\n        wn = sum(abs(c) * (fmpq(N) ** k) for k, c in enumerate(coeffs))\n        vals = [p_poly(fmpq(k)) for k in range(m + 1)]\n        roots_zero = all(vals[k] == 0 for k in range(1, r + 1))\n        max_tail = max(abs(vals[k]) for k in range(r, m + 1))\n        denom_ok = bool(denom >= fmpq(m ** (4 * r), 2))\n        tail_ok = bool(max_tail <= fmpq(2) / (fmpq(m) ** (3 * r)))\n        wn_ok = bool(wn <= ((1 + fmpq(N)) ** r) * ((11 * (1 + fmpq(N))) ** ell))\n        out = {\n            'degree': deg,\n            'denom_ok': denom_ok,\n            'ell': ell,\n            'max_tail': str(max_tail),\n            'p0': str(vals[0]),\n            'roots_zero': roots_zero,\n            'tail_ok': tail_ok,\n            'wn': str(wn),\n            'wn_ok': wn_ok\n        }\n        print(json.dumps(out, sort_keys=True))\nexcept Exception:\n    print(json.dumps({'error': 'preconditions'}, sort_keys=True))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | Quantum circuit compilation and synthesis software can use the parity, symmetric-function, and Dicke-state preparation obstructions as specification-level impossibility constraints for shallow circuit synthesis under the measured-output model. This is an inferred design constraint; an executable software implementation or runtime contract is ABSENT. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper establishes operator-norm localization for conjugated product projections and circuit lower bounds on $\mathcal H_N=(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes N}$. Game mechanics, interactive dynamics, or physical simulation procedures are ABSENT. |
| tools | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A circuit verification or complexity-analysis tool can incorporate the explicit backward scale recurrence and the symmetric-function and state-preparation obstructions to rule out shallow circuit realizations in the stated reflection and Toffoli gate models. A standalone software tool interface is ABSENT. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The paper explicitly constructs the low-degree Chebyshev-root polynomial approximating the zero spectral projection with controlled weighted coefficient norm, the finite backward scale recurrence, and the single-layer block reflection reduction for Dicke states, while establishing algorithmic impossibility for shallow quantum parity and majority circuits. Classical runtime and space complexity bounds for evaluating the auxiliary constructions are ABSENT. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper represents quantum states, product projections, and mismatch count operators algebraically on $\mathcal H_N=(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes N}$. Concrete memory layouts, query structures, or data-structure maintenance bounds are ABSENT. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The circuit model abstracts quantum computation into idealized unitary layers of one-qubit gates and unbounded-arity Toffoli gates. Hardware accelerator microarchitecture, physical qubit routing, or device-level timing contracts are ABSENT. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves unconditional circuit lower bounds for parity and symmetric functions in constant-depth quantum circuits. Cryptographic primitives, secret-key or public-key protocols, and adversarial security reductions are ABSENT. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Although Corollary 2 proves qualitative superpolynomial Fourier concentration for the expected measured sign of constant-depth circuits, learning algorithms, hypothesis classes, training procedures, and statistical generalization bounds are ABSENT. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper uses polynomial approximations and spectral projections to establish quantum circuit lower bounds. Optimization models, mathematical programming formulations, and operations-research scheduling or routing contracts are ABSENT. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Parallelism in the paper refers strictly to unitary gates with pairwise disjoint qubit supports within a single $N$-qubit register. Distributed computing protocols, network partitions, or fault-tolerant consensus mechanisms are ABSENT. |
| other (Quantum circuit complexity, operator localization, and Boolean Fourier analysis) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The paper directly resolves Moore's parity conjecture in the measured-output model ($\mathop{\mathrm{PARITY}}\notin\mathrm{QAC}^0$), proves uniform operator-norm localization for conjugated product projections in $\mathcal U_d(N)$, and derives unconditional bounds on state-preparation felinity $\mathcal F_n(\rho)$, trace distance to weight-$k$ Dicke states $\lvert D_k^n\rangle$, scalar-output Fourier tail weight, and worst-case and average-case computation of strict majority $\operatorname{MAJ}_n(x)=\mathbf 1\{\|x\|>n/2\}$ and symmetric Boolean functions. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The circuit model is restricted to unitary circuits built from arbitrary one-qubit unitaries and unbounded-arity Toffoli gates with disjoint supports in each layer, zero-initialized ancillas $\lvert 0\rangle$, and a single measured output qubit; intermediate measurements, postselection, and primitive unbounded-fanout gates are excluded. Blocked use: ['Applying the parity or majority lower bounds to quantum circuits that use intermediate measurements, adaptive feed-forward, postselection, or primitive unbounded-fanout gates.', 'Applying the layer-depth bound when gates within the same parallel layer have overlapping qubit supports.']
- **L2** (formal_scope): The parity lower bound requires a fixed polynomial upper bound $n^c$ on the total number of qubits $N$ so that exponents $0<s<t<1/c$ satisfy $N^t\le n^{ct}<n$ for sufficiently large $n$; no parity lower bound is established when the ancillary register is allowed to be superpolynomial. Blocked use: ['Claiming that Theorem 1 rules out constant-depth parity computation or approximation when the circuit is allowed superpolynomially many ancillary qubits.', 'Applying the parity contradiction without a polynomial bound relating total qubits $N$ to input length $n$.']
- **L3** (nonconstructive): Every main lower bound and localization estimate holds only for all sufficiently large $n$ or $N$, with an existential asymptotic threshold that depends on the fixed parameters $d,s,t$; no explicit finite threshold on $n$ or $N$ is computed. Blocked use: ['Certifying impossibility or numerical operator-norm bounds at a concrete small finite input length $n$ or qubit count $N$ directly from the asymptotic statements.', 'Treating the sufficient lower bound on $N$ as uniform over all real exponent pairs simultaneously without fixing the gap $t-s$.']
- **L4** (approximation): The polynomial $p$ of Lemma 1 approximates the zero projection $[D=0]$ with error at most $\exp(-N^a)$ only at positive integer eigenvalues up to the ceiling of $N^b$; it makes no accuracy claim at noninteger points between $0$ and $r$ or at eigenvalues exceeding $N^b$. Blocked use: ['Using the polynomial $p$ as a uniform approximation to the indicator of zero on continuous intervals between $0$ and $r$.', 'Replacing $Q$ by $p(C)$ above the spectral threshold $N^b$ without the separate inverse-circuit tail bound.']
- **L5** (hypothesis): Single backward scale induction steps require the strict parameter ordering $0\le s<t$ and $t<b<2t-s$, and operator ordering in $\left\lVert HQP\right\rVert^2=\left\lVert HQPQH\right\rVert$ is noncommutative: only the leftmost $Q$ is replaced by $p(C)$ so the high-count error acts on $PQ$. Blocked use: ['Jumping across a scale interval violating $t<b<2t-s$ in a single polynomial bootstrap step.', 'Commuting $P$ with $C$ or $Q$ when decomposing the approximation error $(Q-p(C))PQ$.']
- **L6** (formal_scope): The state-preparation obstructions in Corollary 1 hold when total qubits are at most $n^c$ in the number $n$ of target output qubits and Dicke weights satisfy $n^\delta\le k\le n/2$; exact constant-depth preparation of every $n$-qubit pure state is possible with $2^{O(n)}$ ancillas, and low-weight Dicke states below $n^\delta$ are not excluded. Blocked use: ['Claiming that constant-depth circuits cannot prepare $n$-qubit pure states when exponential $2^{O(n)}$ ancillas are available (or equivalently when $n$ is logarithmic in the total number of qubits).', 'Applying the Dicke-state trace-distance obstruction $\\operatorname{TD}(\\rho,\\lvert D_k^n\\rangle\\!\\langle D_k^n\\rvert)\\le1/(80k)$ to constant Hamming weights outside $n^\\delta\\le k\\le n/2$.']
- **L7** (formal_scope): The worst-case symmetric-function lower bound (Corollary 3) requires polynomial transition radius $\rho(f_n)\ge n^\delta$ or approximate degree $\widetilde{\deg}_{1/3}(f_n)=\Omega(n^{1/2+\eta})$, and ceases to hold if unit-cost reversible unbounded-arity threshold gates are adjoined to the gate set. Blocked use: ['Applying Corollary 3 to symmetric Boolean functions whose transition radius and approximate degree lie below the polynomial thresholds.', 'Extending the $\\mathrm{QAC}^0$ majority lower bound to quantum circuit models equipped with primitive unbounded-arity threshold gates.']
- **L8** (edge_case): The average-case majority obstruction (Corollary 4) rules out uniform-input average success at least $1-\tfrac12 n^{-\delta}$ for fixed $\delta>0$, but does not assert a constant average-case gap or exclude $1-1/\operatorname{polylog}n$ correlation. Blocked use: ['Claiming that $\\mathrm{QAC}^0$ circuits cannot approximate strict majority on average with $1-1/\\operatorname{polylog}n$ correlation or constant advantage over random guessing.', 'Conflating the worst-case advantage threshold $1/2+1/(\\log n)^\\gamma$ of Corollary 3 with the uniform-input average success threshold $1-\\tfrac12 n^{-\\delta}$ of Corollary 4.']
- **L9** (formal_scope): In Corollary 2, the function $\gamma_d$ is defined nonconstructively via the supremum over all allowed depth-at-most-$d$ circuits and sizes $k\le n\le N$, and $W_{\ge k}[f_C]=\sum_{\|S\|\ge k}\|\widehat f_C(S)\|^2$ is the absolute squared Fourier tail rather than a fraction of $\mathbb E_x f_C(x)^2\le1$. Blocked use: ['Treating $\\gamma_d$ as an explicitly closed-form elementary function of $d$ and $k$.', 'Interpreting the squared Fourier tail as a normalized relative fraction of Fourier weight when $\\mathbb E_x f_C(x)^2\\le1$ is small.']
- **L10** (formal_scope): Unlike the companion article on regular trajectories and pruning, the present operator-norm localization theorem is proved without a trajectory hypothesis, and finite checks of the Chebyshev polynomial or scale chain do not by themselves certify the universal operator-norm induction over all $U\in\mathcal U_d(N)$. Blocked use: ['Requiring regular-trajectory hypotheses to apply Theorem 2.', 'Treating a finite numerical verification of the Chebyshev polynomial or scale chain as a full formal proof of Theorem 1 or Theorem 2.']
- **L11** (unknown): Explicit non-asymptotic formulas for the large-$N$ thresholds in $d,s,t$, closed-form expressions for the universal Fourier decay function $\gamma_d$, average-case majority bounds in the $1-1/\operatorname{polylog}n$ correlation regime, and concrete software-catalogue system implementations are ABSENT from the source corpus. Blocked use: ['Inventing explicit threshold constants for $N$ or closed-form expressions for $\\gamma_d$.', 'Claiming a verified software-catalogue implementation or concrete physical hardware benchmark from this paper.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 274 (`lean/docs/274.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Product-projection localization and the $\mathrm{QAC}^0$ parity lower bound](../../preprints/Product-projection-localization-and-the-QAC0-parity-lower-bound-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Regular trajectories, pruning and quantum parity](../../preprints/Regular-trajectories-pruning-and-quantum-parity-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization rules out bounded-error parity computation by constant-depth quantum circuits with polynomially many zero-initialized ancillary qubits. For every fixed depth, polynomial bound on the total number of qubits, and $0<\varepsilon\le1/2$, every sufficiently large input length has an input on which the measured-output parity success probability is less than $1/2+\varepsilon$. A companion specialization gives success strictly below $2/3$. The formalization also includes the product-projection localization estimate supporting this bound.
>
> The formalized result supplies the polynomial-size parity consequence of the paper. For every fixed circuit depth and polynomial bound on the number of qubits, all sufficiently large input lengths have an input on which any such circuit computes measured-output parity with probability strictly below $2/3$. Ancillary qubits start at zero. The formalization also contains the general positive-advantage parity bound and its product-projection localization estimate; regular-trajectory propagation and pruning are not separate formalized statements here.

Comparator source `QACParity.lean` (2720 bytes, sha256 `9fcf0b33c4236d5feed8a0f7c9b957fa2992cbe68759bf1cfac8e3e9e15d3828`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section
open scoped BigOperators Matrix.Norms.L2Operator

namespace QAC

abbrev Bit := Fin 2
abbrev Word (N : ℕ) := Fin N → Bit
abbrev Operator (N : ℕ) := Matrix (Word N) (Word N) ℂ
abbrev QubitOperator := Matrix Bit Bit ℂ

/-- Tensor product in the computational basis. -/
def tensorMatrix {N : ℕ} (a : Fin N → QubitOperator) : Operator N :=
  fun x y => ∏ i, a i (x i) (y i)

/-- A one-qubit unitary or an unbounded-arity Toffoli gate. -/
inductive Gate (N : ℕ) where
  | local (q : Fin N) (u : Matrix.unitaryGroup Bit ℂ)
  | toffoli (controls : Finset (Fin N)) (target : Fin N) (hne : target ∉ controls)

namespace Gate

def support {N : ℕ} : Gate N → Finset (Fin N)
  | .local q _ => {q}
  | .toffoli c t _ => insert t c

/-- A Toffoli flips its target iff all controls are one. -/
def toffoliWord {N : ℕ} (c : Finset (Fin N)) (t : Fin N) (x : Word N) : Word N :=
  if ∀ i ∈ c, x i = 1 then Function.update x t (1 - x t) else x

def matrix {N : ℕ} : Gate N → Operator N
  | .local q u => tensorMatrix fun i => if i = q then (u : QubitOperator) else 1
  | .toffoli c t _ => fun x y => if x = toffoliWord c t y then 1 else 0

end Gate

structure PhysicalLayer (N : ℕ) where
  gates : List (Gate N)
  disjoint : gates.Pairwise (fun A B => Disjoint A.support B.support)

def physicalLayerMatrix {N : ℕ} (L : PhysicalLayer N) : Operator N :=
  (L.gates.map Gate.matrix).prod

/-- Chronological order: the rightmost matrix acts first. -/
def physicalCircuitMatrix {N : ℕ} (layers : List (PhysicalLayer N)) : Operator N :=
  ((layers.map physicalLayerMatrix).reverse).prod

/-- The computational-basis input with the remaining qubits initialized to zero. -/
def inputWord {n N : ℕ} (x : Word n) : Word N :=
  fun i => if h : i.val < n then x ⟨i.val, h⟩ else 0

def parity {n : ℕ} (x : Word n) : Bit := ∑ i, x i

/-- Born probability, summing all final garbage with no cleanliness assumption. -/
def successProbability {n N : ℕ} (W : Operator N) (out : Fin N) (x : Word n) : ℝ :=
  ∑ y : Word N, if y out = parity x then Complex.normSq (W y (inputWord x)) else 0

/-- The literal measured-output, polynomial-total-qubit parity lower bound. -/
def ParityStatement : Prop :=
  ∀ (d : ℕ) (c ε : ℝ), 1 ≤ c → 0 < ε → ε ≤ 1 / 2 →
    ∃ n₀ : ℕ, ∀ n ≥ n₀, ∀ N : ℕ, n ≤ N → (N : ℝ) ≤ (n : ℝ) ^ c →
      ∀ layers : List (PhysicalLayer N), layers.length ≤ d →
        ∀ out : Fin N, ¬ (∀ x : Word n,
          1 / 2 + ε ≤ successProbability (physicalCircuitMatrix layers) out x)

end QAC

theorem QAC.parity_lower_bound : QAC.ParityStatement := by
  sorry

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that constant-depth quantum circuits with arbitrary one-qubit and unbounded-arity Toffoli gates cannot compute parity with any fixed positive worst-case advantage using polynomially many qubits. Ancillas start in zero, only one output qubit is measured, and all final garbage is unrestricted. This resolves Moore's parity conjecture in the measured-output model.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
